### T0f3896c00cfd09efc1e0f4ed

Text hash e56e48c5a3f35aa5a92bdb7bb2370d9d8c97fc45051aa3355f3d6c57e87d1b8b; absolute characters 0–726; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8555-exemption-samuel-cottrell-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Samuel A. Cottrell, auditor, Income Tax Unit, Bureau of Internal Revenue, Treasury Department, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 8262 of September 26, 1939, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of nine months:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Samuel A. Cottrell from compulsory retirement for age for a period of nine months ending June 30, 1941.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

September 28, 1940.

```

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Louis A. Simon, Supervising Architect of the Public Buildings Administration, Federal Works Agency, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for periods totaling four years by Executive Orders No. 7581 of March 19, 1937, No. 7824 of February 25, 1938, No. 8046 of February 11, 1939, and No. 8369 of March 7, 1940, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of six months:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Louis A. Simon from compulsory retirement for age for a period of six months ending November 30, 1941.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

April 28, 1941.

```

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Ales Hrdlicka, Curator of Physical Anthropology in the United States National Museum, Smithsonian Institution, who, during the month of March, 1939, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Ales Hrdlicka from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending March 31, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

February 23, 1939.

```

### Tb971b0163179fae2494691a0

Text hash 27394db2d00c56f5fef06b104b510410d7b6879df822da81254abe968245c38f; absolute characters 0–1338; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7824-exemption-louis-simon-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *";

And Whereas the public interest requires that Louis A. Simon, supervising architect of the Public Buildings Branch of the Procurement Division, Treasury Department, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7581 of March 19, 1937, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until June 1, 1939;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Louis A. Simon from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until June 1, 1939.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

February 25, 1938.

```

```text
The White House,

May 7, 1940.
```

### Te52cf0db94806f44ff5d99fa

Text hash 4646703c57647c16e24531c93bb8de324ad8c14adfd3e143d98d84f44d8e332f; absolute characters 0–1331; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7581-exemption-louis-simon-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *";

And Whereas the public interest requires that Louis A. Simon, supervising architect of the Public Buildings Branch of the Procurement Division, Treasury Department, who, during the month of May, 1937, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until June 1, 1938;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Louis A. Simon from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until June 1, 1938.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

March 19, 1937.

```

Whole-order status is no (provisional, already adopted; not changed here). The order exempts Hervey G. The order exempts Frank Burke from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending December 31, 1941 and ends on that stated date. The end is the order's own fixed term, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. Held later events: EO 9002, a further exemption for a later period, recorded as a later event and not as an end. Savings: accrued effects of service during the period. Whole-order status is no (provisional, already adopted; not changed here). The order exempts James C. Wynde from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending November 30, 1941 and ends on that stated date. The end is the order's own fixed term, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. No later event is held. Nothing in the held evidence shows the exemption ended before September 30, 1942.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The recital states the public-interest finding and the operative sentence is the only command; the signature and date add none. Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-11-04: the APP mirror page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (Common Crawl capture 2025-10-05T15:30:25Z) has the heading \"Executive Order 8796—Exemption of George Cook From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", its date (1941-06-18) and subject agree with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Identity: the register lists this number with date 1941-06-18, signer Franklin D. Roosevelt and the title \"Exemption of George Cook From Compulsory Retirement for Age\"; the held text concerns George Cook and ends with the signature \"FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\" and the date line \"June 18, 1941.\". Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy.\nContent: the order has one recital and one operative sentence. It exempts one named employee, George Cook (plate printer, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury Department), from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), for a period of one year ending July 31, 1942. The recital says he \"will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service\" and asks that he \"be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year\". The inventory is therefore one clause, the exemption. Extension reading: this order is the initial exemption; any later exemption of the same person would be a new exercise of the section 204 power with its own period and a later event, not an end of this order, and the packet's searches show none in the held texts.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The only cessation source is the order's own period, \\\"one year ending July 31, 1942\\\". The packet's bounded number search of the held order texts (lookup scope: exactly held_texts(), clause-drafts excluded) found no other held text that cites Executive Order 8796. The Federal Register catalog disposition-note search (later-events:ofr_catalog, checked 2026-10-19) returned no hit for this order. The person-name search of the held order texts (namier:held_texts_person_name, checked 2026-10-28, whose leads list later or undated orders only) found no later order naming George Cook. Statute check (the type rule research/readiness/type-rules/TR-RETIREMENT-EXEMPTION.json lists a change in the governing retirement statute as a possible earlier end): held APP exemption orders signed after this one still rest on the same section, for example EO 9271 of November 17, 1942 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said George T. Summerlin from compulsory retirement for age\\\") and EO 9530 of March 14, 1945 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry F. Hawley from compulsory retirement for age\\\"). Their wording shows that section 204 was still the authority for exemptions after this order's last day, so it had not been repealed. No held source shows that any amendment of section 204 extended or extinguished this order's dated exemption, and a later general rule is an end only with a reason it extinguished the command (owner ruling 2026-10-28). The employee's separation, retirement or death would end the practical use of the exemption but is not an instrument ending the command, and none is cited in the packet. Amendments are later events, not ends, and none is found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None found. The order contains one command, the exemption of one named employee; it prescribes no report, procedure, delegation, appropriation or continuing duty. The compulsory-retirement rule it excepts comes from section 204 and the retirement statutes, which are independent of this order, so when the exemption's period ended no command of this order remained to continue.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption is exhausted at 1942-07-31; its accrued effects (service, pay, retirement credit) are separate from the order and are stated as transferred_or_accrued. No later exemption order for the same person is found by the name search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: one named employee, George Cook (plate printer, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury Department). The dossier inventory has one whole-order clause (characters 0-660 of the registered file, the recital and the operative sentence); the signature and date line follow it in the full 695-character text. Nothing in the text is outside that clause, so no inventory correction is needed.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8796#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:8796#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"629adb8be1fdb30a7591296dd003f58b1ed9c71263f44de6771848c7510115a5","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8796-exemption-george-cook-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"629adb8be1fdb30a7591296dd003f58b1ed9c71263f44de6771848c7510115a5","state":"match"},"sha256":"95f4757d30ee4af2a6194b06d415d59e2da418efbab596da06a01fc43e1c9157","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8796-exemption-george-cook-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"629adb8be1fdb30a7591296dd003f58b1ed9c71263f44de6771848c7510115a5"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"629adb8be1fdb30a7591296dd003f58b1ed9c71263f44de6771848c7510115a5","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8796-exemption-george-cook-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"629adb8be1fdb30a7591296dd003f58b1ed9c71263f44de6771848c7510115a5","state":"match"},"sha256":"95f4757d30ee4af2a6194b06d415d59e2da418efbab596da06a01fc43e1c9157","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8796-exemption-george-cook-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"629adb8be1fdb30a7591296dd003f58b1ed9c71263f44de6771848c7510115a5"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"629adb8be1fdb30a7591296dd003f58b1ed9c71263f44de6771848c7510115a5","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8796-exemption-george-cook-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"629adb8be1fdb30a7591296dd003f58b1ed9c71263f44de6771848c7510115a5","state":"match"},"sha256":"95f4757d30ee4af2a6194b06d415d59e2da418efbab596da06a01fc43e1c9157","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8796-exemption-george-cook-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"62 Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-11-04: the APP mirror page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (Common Crawl capture 2025-10-13T10:08:50Z) has the heading \"Executive Order 8804—Exemption of James E. Harper From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", its date (1941-06-25) and subject agree with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Identity: the register lists this number with date 1941-06-25, signer Franklin D. Roosevelt and the title \"Exemption of James E. Harper From Compulsory Retirement for Age\"; the held text concerns James E. Harper and ends with the signature \"FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\" and the date line \"June 25, 1941.\". Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy.\nContent: the order has one recital and one operative sentence. It exempts one named employee, James E. Harper (Chief, Employee Relations Section, Division of Personnel, Treasury Department), from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), for a period of three months ending September 30, 1941. The recital says he \"was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for periods totaling two years by Executive Orders No. 8139 of May 19, 1939, and No. 8422 of May 28, 1940\" and asks that he \"be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of three months\"; the earlier exemption orders it recites are earlier instruments, not later events, and not ends of this order. The inventory is therefore one clause, the exemption. Extension reading: this order is the further exemption; any later exemption of the same person would be a new exercise of the section 204 power with its own period and a later event, not an end of this order, and the packet's searches show none in the held texts.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The only cessation source is the order's own period, \\\"three months ending September 30, 1941\\\". The packet's bounded by-number search of the held order texts (lookup scope: exactly held_texts(), clause-drafts excluded) returned 0 matches for Executive Order 8804, so no held text cites this order by number. The catalog search of the Office of the Federal Register disposition notes (later-events:ofr_catalog, checked 2026-10-19) is a different search; it reports 2 hits for this order, which are 'See:' cross-references in the later-events edge file (target EO:8804) pairing this order with EO 8139 (May 19, 1939) and EO 8422 (May 28, 1940), the earlier exemption orders recited in the text of this order; they are earlier instruments, not later events. The person-name search of the held order texts (namier:held_texts_person_name, checked 2026-10-28, whose leads list later or undated orders only) found no later order naming James E. Harper. Statute check (the type rule research/readiness/type-rules/TR-RETIREMENT-EXEMPTION.json lists a change in the governing retirement statute as a possible earlier end): held APP exemption orders signed after this one still rest on the same section, for example EO 9271 of November 17, 1942 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said George T. Summerlin from compulsory retirement for age\\\") and EO 9530 of March 14, 1945 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry F. Hawley from compulsory retirement for age\\\"). Their wording shows that section 204 was still the authority for exemptions after this order's last day, so it had not been repealed. No held source shows that any amendment of section 204 extended or extinguished this order's dated exemption, and a later general rule is an end only with a reason it extinguished the command (owner ruling 2026-10-28). The employee's separation, retirement or death would end the practical use of the exemption but is not an instrument ending the command, and none is cited in the packet. Amendments are later events, not ends, and none is found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None found. The order contains one command, the exemption of one named employee; it prescribes no report, procedure, delegation, appropriation or continuing duty. The compulsory-retirement rule it excepts comes from section 204 and the retirement statutes, which are independent of this order, so when the exemption's period ended no command of this order remained to continue.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption is exhausted at 1941-09-30; its accrued effects (service, pay, retirement credit) are separate from the order and are stated as transferred_or_accrued. No later exemption order for the same person is found by the name search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: one named employee, James E. Harper (Chief, Employee Relations Section, Division of Personnel, Treasury Department). grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-11-04: the APP mirror page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (Common Crawl capture 2025-10-09T14:28:41Z) has the heading \"Executive Order 8805—Exemption of Samuel A. Cottrell From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", its date (1941-06-25) and subject agree with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Identity: the register lists this number with date 1941-06-25, signer Franklin D. Roosevelt and the title \"Exemption of Samuel A. Cottrell From Compulsory Retirement for Age\"; the held text concerns Samuel A. Cottrell and ends with the signature \"FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\" and the date line \"June 25, 1941.\". Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy.\nContent: the order has one recital and one operative sentence. It exempts one named employee, Samuel A. Cottrell (auditor, Income Tax Unit, Bureau of Internal Revenue, Treasury Department), from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), for a period of one year ending June 30, 1942. The recital says he \"was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for periods totaling one year and nine months by Executive Orders No. 8262 of September 26, 1939, and No. 8555 of September 28, 1940\" and asks that he \"be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year\"; the earlier exemption orders it recites are earlier instruments, not later events, and not ends of this order. The inventory is therefore one clause, the exemption. Extension reading: this order is the further exemption; any later exemption of the same person would be a new exercise of the section 204 power with its own period and a later event, not an end of this order, and the packet's searches show none in the held texts.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The only cessation source is the order's own period, \\\"one year ending June 30, 1942\\\". The packet's bounded by-number search of the held order texts (lookup scope: exactly held_texts(), clause-drafts excluded) returned 0 matches for Executive Order 8805, so no held text cites this order by number. The catalog search of the Office of the Federal Register disposition notes (later-events:ofr_catalog, checked 2026-10-19) is a different search; it reports 2 hits for this order, which are 'See:' cross-references in the later-events edge file (target EO:8805) pairing this order with EO 8262 (September 26, 1939) and EO 8555 (August 16, 1941 as dated in the note; September 28, 1940 in its own text), the earlier exemption orders recited in the text of this order; they are earlier instruments, not later events. The person-name search of the held order texts (namier:held_texts_person_name, checked 2026-10-28, whose leads list later or undated orders only) found no later order naming Samuel A. Cottrell. Statute check (the type rule research/readiness/type-rules/TR-RETIREMENT-EXEMPTION.json lists a change in the governing retirement statute as a possible earlier end): held APP exemption orders signed after this one still rest on the same section, for example EO 9271 of November 17, 1942 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said George T. Summerlin from compulsory retirement for age\\\") and EO 9530 of March 14, 1945 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry F. Hawley from compulsory retirement for age\\\"). Their wording shows that section 204 was still the authority for exemptions after this order's last day, so it had not been repealed. No held source shows that any amendment of section 204 extended or extinguished this order's dated exemption, and a later general rule is an end only with a reason it extinguished the command (owner ruling 2026-10-28). The employee's separation, retirement or death would end the practical use of the exemption but is not an instrument ending the command, and none is cited in the packet. Amendments are later events, not ends, and none is found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None found. The order contains one command, the exemption of one named employee; it prescribes no report, procedure, delegation, appropriation or continuing duty. The compulsory-retirement rule it excepts comes from section 204 and the retirement statutes, which are independent of this order, so when the exemption's period ended no command of this order remained to continue.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption is exhausted at 1942-06-30; its accrued effects (service, pay, retirement credit) are separate from the order and are stated as transferred_or_accrued. No later exemption order for the same person is found by the name search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: one named employee, Samuel A. Cottrell (auditor, Income Tax Unit, Bureau of Internal Revenue, Treasury Department). The dossier inventory has one whole-order clause (characters 0-738 of the registered file, the recital and the operative sentence); the signature and date line follow it in the full 773-character text. Nothing in the text is outside that clause, so no inventory correction is needed.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8805#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:8805#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"956918b074f2ad3d4edc6126dd8110b29a292890f568be4fb32fbf49ffa226b1","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8805-exemption-samuel-cottrell-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"956918b074f2ad3d4edc6126dd8110b29a292890f568be4fb32fbf49ffa226b1","state":"match"},"sha256":"f3b153cdf952d42810fb90474d13e822eb425f94974e6951320d38a822955658","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8805-exemption-samuel-cottrell-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"956918b074f2ad3d4edc6126dd8110b29a292890f568be4fb32fbf49ffa226b1"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"956918b074f2ad3d4edc6126dd8110b29a292890f568be4fb32fbf49ffa226b1","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8805-exemption-samuel-cottrell-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"956918b074f2ad3d4edc6126dd8110b29a292890f568be4fb32fbf49ffa226b1","state":"match"},"sha256":"f3b153cdf952d42810fb90474d13e822eb425f94974e6951320d38a822955658","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8805-exemption-samuel-cottrell-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"956918b074f2ad3d4edc6126dd8110b29a292890f568be4fb32fbf49ffa226b1"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"956918b074f2ad3d4edc6126dd8110b29a292890f568be4fb32fbf49ffa226b1","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8805-exemption-samuel-cottrell-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"956918b074f2ad3d4edc6126dd8110b29a292890f568be4fb32fbf49ffa226b1","state":"match"},"sha256":"f3b153cdf952d42810fb90474d13e822eb425f94974e6951320d38a822955658","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8805-exemption-samuel-cottrell-from-compulsory-retirement-for-ag Bishop From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", its date (1941-06-30) and subject agree with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Identity: the register lists this number with date 1941-06-30, signer Franklin D. Roosevelt and the title \"Exemption of Hurbert K. Bishop From Compulsory Retirement for Age\"; the held text concerns Hubert K. Bishop and ends with the signature \"FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\" and the date line \"June 30, 1941.\". Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. The register title names \"Hurbert K. Bishop\" while the APP heading and the held text name Hubert K. Bishop; the number, date and one-person subject agree, so the identity holds.\nContent: the order has one recital and one operative sentence. It exempts one named employee, Hubert K. Bishop (Chief, Division of Construction, Public Roads Administration, Federal Works Agency), from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), for a period of one year ending June 30, 1942. The recital says he \"was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 8413 of May 18, 1940\" and asks that he \"be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year\"; the earlier exemption orders it recites are earlier instruments, not later events, and not ends of this order. The inventory is therefore one clause, the exemption. Extension reading: this order is the further exemption; any later exemption of the same person would be a new exercise of the section 204 power with its own period and a later event, not an end of this order, and the packet's searches show none in the held texts.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The only cessation source is the order's own period, \\\"one year ending June 30, 1942\\\". The packet's bounded by-number search of the held order texts (lookup scope: exactly held_texts(), clause-drafts excluded) returned 0 matches for Executive Order 8810, so no held text cites this order by number. The catalog search of the Office of the Federal Register disposition notes (later-events:ofr_catalog, checked 2026-10-19) is a different search; it reports 1 hit for this order, which are 'See:' cross-references in the later-events edge file (target EO:8810) pairing this order with EO 8413 (May 18, 1940), the earlier exemption order recited in the text of this order; they are earlier instruments, not later events. The person-name search of the held order texts (namier:held_texts_person_name, checked 2026-10-28, whose leads list later or undated orders only) found no later order naming Hubert K. Bishop. Statute check (the type rule research/readiness/type-rules/TR-RETIREMENT-EXEMPTION.json lists a change in the governing retirement statute as a possible earlier end): held APP exemption orders signed after this one still rest on the same section, for example EO 9271 of November 17, 1942 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said George T. Summerlin from compulsory retirement for age\\\") and EO 9530 of March 14, 1945 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry F. Hawley from compulsory retirement for age\\\"). Their wording shows that section 204 was still the authority for exemptions after this order's last day, so it had not been repealed. No held source shows that any amendment of section 204 extended or extinguished this order's dated exemption, and a later general rule is an end only with a reason it extinguished the command (owner ruling 2026-10-28). The employee's separation, retirement or death would end the practical use of the exemption but is not an instrument ending the command, and none is cited in the packet. Amendments are later events, not ends, and none is found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None found. The order contains one command, the exemption of one named employee; it prescribes no report, procedure, delegation, appropriation or continuing duty. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy.\nContent: the order has one recital and one operative sentence. It exempts one named employee, Bernard S. Kroger (foreman of plate printing, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury Department), from compulsory retirement for age under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), for a period of one year ending August 31, 1942. The recital says he \"will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service\" and asks that he \"be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year\". The inventory is therefore one clause, the exemption. Extension reading: this order is the initial exemption; any later exemption of the same person would be a new exercise of the section 204 power with its own period and a later event, not an end of this order, and the packet's searches show none in the held texts.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The only cessation source is the order's own period, \\\"one year ending August 31, 1942\\\". The packet's bounded number search of the held order texts (lookup scope: exactly held_texts(), clause-drafts excluded) found no other held text that cites Executive Order 8825. The Federal Register catalog disposition-note search (later-events:ofr_catalog, checked 2026-10-19) returned no hit for this order. The person-name search of the held order texts (namier:held_texts_person_name, checked 2026-10-28, whose leads list later or undated orders only) found no later order naming Bernard S. Kroger. Statute check (the type rule research/readiness/type-rules/TR-RETIREMENT-EXEMPTION.json lists a change in the governing retirement statute as a possible earlier end): held APP exemption orders signed after this one still rest on the same section, for example EO 9271 of November 17, 1942 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said George T. Summerlin from compulsory retirement for age\\\") and EO 9530 of March 14, 1945 (\\\"section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry F. Hawley from compulsory retirement for age\\\"). Their wording shows that section 204 was still the authority for exemptions after this order's last day, so it had not been repealed. No held source shows that any amendment of section 204 extended or extinguished this order's dated exemption, and a later general rule is an end only with a reason it extinguished the command (owner ruling 2026-10-28). The employee's separation, retirement or death would end the practical use of the exemption but is not an instrument ending the command, and none is cited in the packet. Amendments are later events, not ends, and none is found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None found. The order contains one command, the exemption of one named employee; it prescribes no report, procedure, delegation, appropriation or continuing duty. The compulsory-retirement rule it excepts comes from section 204 and the retirement statutes, which are independent of this order, so when the exemption's period ended no command of this order remained to continue.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption is exhausted at 1942-08-31; its accrued effects (service, pay, retirement credit) are separate from the order and are stated as transferred_or_accrued. No later exemption order for the same person is found by the name search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: one named employee, Bernard S. Kroger (foreman of plate printing, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury Department). The dossier inventory has one whole-order clause (characters 0-688 of the registered file, the recital and the operative sentence); the signature and date line follow it in the full 723-character text. Nothing in the text is outside that clause, so no inventory correction is ## Valla review leads and component attestations

Review lead only. Every case needs separate applicability, earlier-ending, scope, independent-command, savings and independent whole-case review.
Preparation attestations never approve legal conclusions or replace whole-case review.
